# Decision ledger: Eve's numbered decisions (tracked)

Status: written 2026-11-16 by task DECISION-RULES-1 from the ledger of the review session (`tmpAI/panel/adr/ledger.md`, git-ignored, updated 2026-11-15 23:30) plus her decisions of the agent channel from M139 to M154 of 2026-11-16. **The PATH of this file (`.claude/knowledge/decision_ledger.md`) was proposed (M150 point 5 gave it only as an example) and Eve kept it on 2026-11-16: "8. both defaults." (`D-0158`).**

## What this file is

The one place where every decision of Eve has a NUMBER that a script can read (`D-0001`, `D-0002`, ...). A brief quotes numbers from here (`.claude/workflows/decision_compliance.md`); the hook `.claude/hooks/coordinator_guard.py` refuses a builder or a reviewer call whose brief cites a number that does not exist; `assets/scripts/decision_ledger.py` reads this file (`list`, `check D-0001 ...`).

## Line format

One decision per line, ten fields separated by " | " (a line that starts with `D-` and a four-digit number is a decision; every other line is text):

`number | date | area | decision | source | taken by | status | relation | her words and evidence | old key`

- **taken by**: `EVE` = she decided or reviewed it, with the record quoted. `AGENT` = an agent or the coordinator chose it. `NOT STATED` = the record gives a decision without saying who took it. `REVIEW SESSION` = a plan that the review session relayed in the agent channel and that she did not say in her own words. Nothing is assumed.
- **status**: only a line taken by EVE is `DECIDED`. An AGENT line is `PROVISIONAL` (it goes back to her). A NOT STATED line is `NOT-STATED` (not a decision until she confirms it). A REVIEW SESSION line is `RELAYED` (a plan, not decided until she confirms it; it names the decided line it was split from). The script refuses a line whose status does not follow its "taken by".
- **relation**: `-` when the record names none. A later decision names the number it replaces (`replaces D-NNNN`, `replaced in part by D-NNNN`, `refines`, `refined by`, `consistent with`). A reversal is also a decision and gets its own line.
- **old key**: the key of the line in the earlier ledger (`L01`...`L47`, `N01`...`N52`) or the channel block of the new lines (`M140-a`): the texts of the old lines still name old keys (for example "replaced by N01"); this field maps them to the number.
- Dates are written in full (`2026-11-05`); the old ledger wrote `11-05`.
- **Who writes a line**: a scribe, the same day the decision is taken, with her words quoted. Nobody rewrites an existing line; a change of mind is a new line that names the one it replaces. (One exception, 2026-11-16, on the review of this file: `D-0108` to `D-0129` were rewritten once so that each holds only her words; the parts that were the review session's own moved to `D-0130` to `D-0142`; a number was never reused for another text. A second exception, 2026-11-16 (Eve, `D-0161`): a relayed line that she then confirmed changed its taken by, status and relation only (the relation ends with "; confirmed by D-0161"; `D-0140` ends with "; replaced by D-0141 (recorded by D-0161)"); its decision words and every other field did not change. `app/tests/test_decision_ledger.py` allows exactly this and nothing else.)

D-0266 | 2026-11-18 | step_, parameters, tests, docs | How a `step_` passes parameters (Variant 1). A `step_` declares the `polars_` steps it composes (for example `composes: [polars_str_split, polars_list_to_struct, polars_fill_null]`) and takes ONE NESTED BLOCK per composed step, named after that Polars step, holding that step's own parameters with Polars' own names. An absent optional block means that step is not run. The step's own additions (for example the checks `too_many` and `too_few`) only stop or warn; they never change a value. Tests: a registry test fails when a passed parameter name is not a parameter of the composed Polars step (with a did-you-mean); an equality test proves the `step_` gives the same table as the explicit chain of `polars_` steps, with and without the optional blocks. Documentation of `step_` and `modif_` is written with special care (the meaning of each name, the equality rule, nested blocks, off when absent, examples with the explicit chain beside the step, what the step adds beyond Polars, a table of every `step_` and `modif_` with the Polars steps behind it). The Blueprint help for them is DEFERRED. The rule is applied to the existing `step_` actions in the design (each shows its `composes` list, and what would change is reported); not built. Example in M281: `step_split_wide` with the blocks `str_split` and `fill_null`. | channel M281 of 57 (2026-11-18 10:58) | EVE | DECIDED | refines D-0091, D-0210, D-0254 | Her words, M281: "the idea is to reproduce the tidyverse behavior with polars step_ so we need to be able to pass the correct parameters to the step_" and "ok variant 1 - but needs to be documented properly we need special care to document how to use the step_ and modif_ in the documentation". D-0346 | 2026-11-20 | HOME audit | The tick lines of the audit dialog: only distinct tables are lines. An empty tier is not a separate place: a join without tier 2 steps is the same table as its tier 1, a plot without tier 3 steps reads the same table as tier 2; the line is the lowest level of the lineage that really has steps. Each line shows its reach: how many tables and plots depend on it; a step on a shared table reaches everything below it. There is also a line for one plot only: the audit copy gives the plot its own audit steps at tier 3 even when the recipe has no tier 3 steps for it; its reach is one plot. A join that no plot reads is not offered (question Q-A17). To test: the reach count and the collapse of identical places, with a plot without tier 3 steps, a join without tier 2 steps and a table read by several plots.
